The derivative of a constant with respect to any variable is
A. Undefined
B. Equal to the constant itself
C. Zero
D. Equal to one
Answer: C

The rate of change of velocity with respect to time is called
A. Displacement
B. Speed
C. Acceleration
D. Momentum
Answer: C

In engineering economy, the depreciation method that assumes an equal annual reduction in an asset's book value over its useful life is the
A. Sum-of-years-digits method
B. Straight-line method
C. Declining balance method
D. Sinking fund method
Answer: B

The equivalent value of a series of future cash flows expressed in terms of today's money is called the
A. Future worth
B. Present worth
C. Annual worth
D. Salvage value
Answer: B

In a set of data, the value that occurs with the greatest frequency is called the
A. Mean
B. Median
C. Mode
D. Range
Answer: C

Newton's second law of motion states that the net force acting on a body is equal to
A. Its mass divided by acceleration
B. Its mass multiplied by acceleration
C. Its weight multiplied by velocity
D. Its momentum divided by time
Answer: B

The SI unit of work and energy is the
A. Watt
B. Newton
C. Joule
D. Pascal
Answer: C

The first law of thermodynamics is fundamentally a statement of the principle of
A. Conservation of mass
B. Conservation of energy
C. Entropy increase
D. Conservation of momentum
Answer: B

The ratio of the circumference of a circle to its diameter is the constant
A. e
B. pi
C. phi
D. Euler's constant
Answer: B

In economic analysis, the point at which total revenue equals total cost is called the
A. Payback period
B. Break-even point
C. Rate of return
D. Depreciation point
Answer: B

A physical quantity that has both magnitude and direction is called a
A. Scalar
B. Vector
C. Tensor
D. Constant
Answer: B

The logarithm of 1, to any valid base, is always equal to
A. Undefined
B. Negative one
C. Zero
D. The base itself
Answer: C

B. The time value of money
C. Opportunity cost
D. Sunk cost
Answer: B

The SI unit used to express electrical resistance is the
A. Ampere
B. Volt
C. Ohm
D. Watt
Answer: C

Pascal's principle states that pressure applied to a confined, incompressible fluid is
A. Absorbed entirely at the point of application
B. Transmitted undiminished to every point of the fluid and to the walls of the container
C. Reduced proportionally with distance from the point of application
D. Converted entirely into kinetic energy
Answer: B

A. pi
B. e
C. i
D. phi
Answer: B

In engineering economy, the interest rate at which the present worth of a project's cash inflows equals the present worth of its cash outflows is called the
A. Nominal interest rate
B. Effective interest rate
C. Internal rate of return (IRR)
D. Minimum attractive rate of return (MARR)
Answer: C

A. Amortization
B. Depreciation
C. Escalation
D. Capitalization
Answer: B

A. Median

Hooke's law states that within the limits of elasticity the displacement produced in a body is proportional to the force applied. That is F = kx, where k is called modulus. Thus, if the modulus of a spring is 20 pounds per inch, the work required to stretch or compress the spring a distance of 6 inches is
A. 30 in-lb
B. 32 in-lb
C. 35 in-lb
D. 55 in-lb

D. 925 rev
Answer: C

An elevator weighing 5000 lb attains an upward velocity of 25 fps in 5 sec with uniform acceleration. What is the tension in the supporting cable in lbs?
A. 4776.4 lbs
B. 5776.4 lbs
C. 6776.4 lbs
D. 7776.4 lbs
Answer: B

B. θ = 12.2°
C. θ = 22.2°
D. θ = 32.2°
Answer: B

A. 3.3 mi/hr
B. 4.3 mi/hr
C. 5.3 mi/hr
D. 6.3 mi/hr
Answer: A

A. 1.93 ft
B. 2.25 ft
C. 5 ft
D. 6.25 ft
Answer: A

A. 0.75
B. 0.81
C. 0.92
D. 0.99
Answer: B

B. 58 lb
C. 48 lb
D. 77 lb
Answer: B

B. 208 lb
C. 195 lb
D. 280 lb
Answer: B

A freely falling body is a body in rectilinear motion and with constant ______.
A. Velocity
B. Speed
C. Deceleration
D. Acceleration
Answer: D

A. Static collision
B. Elastic collision
C. Inelastic collision
D. Plastic collision
Answer: B

A. Transverse harmonic motion
B. Resonance
C. Rotational harmonic motion
D. Translational harmonic motion
Answer: D

Impulse causes ______.
A. The object's momentum to change
B. The object's momentum to decrease
C. The object's momentum to increase
D. The object's momentum to remain constant or to be conserved
Answer: A

B. Dynamics

D. Kinematics
